a:T491,The analysis is set in the continuous-time Markov chain formulation of discrete diffusion. A transition-rate matrix defines the forward noising process and a family of marginal distributions p_t. The reverse process requires concrete scores, i.e. pairwise ratios p_t(y)/p_t(x). SEDD learns these ratios with the score-entropy loss, a weighted Bregman divergence D(a,b)=a/b-1-log(a/b), where the weights are induced by the transition structure.

The main contribution is a minimax treatment of this estimation problem. The paper first derives a lower bound for estimating a single concrete score function under the score-entropy risk. It then constructs a simple estimator, essentially a smoothed or clipped empirical plug-in estimator for the relevant probability ratios, and proves that its risk nearly matches the lower bound. The single-time result is then connected back to SEDD by controlling the time-averaged score-estimation term that appears in KL guarantees for the generated distribution. This complements analyses of tau-leaping, uniformization, Gillespie, or Euler samplers, which study how to simulate the reverse chain once accurate scores are available.b:T4bb,A sampling-based watermark adapted to diffusion decoding.
- Injection: replace position-indexed i.i.d. Gumbel perturbations with a smooth, locally auto-correlated Gumbel field (SAC). The field is constructed via a Gaussian copula so that each coordinate keeps Gumbel marginals (under the paper’s assumptions) while introducing controlled local dependence across neighboring positions.
- Rationale: diffusion decoding refines multiple positions in parallel; independent high-frequency perturbations across neighbors can conflict with local consistency during iterative updates, causing oscillations or incoherence.
- Mechanistic analysis: quantify “latent perturbation roughness” via a local-difference energy and show how positive low-lag dependence reduces this roughness (the paper provides an analytic form contrasting i.i.d. vs SAC).
- Detection: a SAC-aware detector using covariance-aware filtering plus native-sample calibration to manage false positives. A global-offset scan is added as a coarse synchronization recovery tool under mild token–signal misalignment.
Overall, it modifies keyed randomness during DLM decoding and pairs it with a matched detector, rather than changing DLM training.c:T449,The diagnostic pair is a Qwen3.5-27B mentor and a Qwen3.5-4B student. On Multi-IF, linear probes on mentor states show a large freshness gap: stale-state accuracy falls from 0.79 at t=0 to 0.47 at t=512, while fresh-state accuracy stays between 0.76 and 0.80. This drift also affects behavior. Static guidance reduces the 4B student’s constraint satisfaction by 2.5 points below the no-guidance baseline, while refreshing every 16 tokens turns it into a 2.0-point gain over that baseline. Fresh but mismatched memory does not help, which supports the content-freshness explanation. Across 13 datasets, MentorPulse closes 52.2% of the mentor-student gap on the main pair, roughly twice the recovery of the strongest prior alternative. Gains concentrate on long-output tasks. Short outputs often finish before refresh matters, and very large mentor-student capability gaps reduce the margin. The proposed V64 read-distribution indicator helps screen pairs before deployment. Throughput measurements place R=16 among the efficient settings tested against text-to-text guidance on long-output tasks.d:T7ab,方法分成两部分：TDES 和 SABA。

TDES（Target-Distilled Edge Scoring）解决父子错配。现有 DDTree 的边选择近似是 parent-agnostic 的：child token 在不同 parent 下贡献相同，只由该位置的 token probability 决定。GRAFT 改成显式给 edge 打分。做法是从 target model 的验证轨迹里蒸馏一个轻量 edge scorer，输入是相邻位置候选节点及其上下文特征，输出该 parent-child transition 被 target 接受的兼容性分数。然后树构建时不再只按各位置 token 概率乘积选路径，而是结合 edge score 进行 parent-conditional 组装。这样保留了 DLM 一次前向产出候选节点的优点，又补上了“节点之间是否能连成 target 偏好的路径”这一层信息。

从机制上看，这一步很像把 target 的局部条件分布偏好压缩成一个便宜的结构预测器。它不是重新跑 target，也不是让 drafter 变成 AR，而是在 assembly 阶段补一个 learned compatibility model。相比直接增大候选集，这种做法更有效，因为问题不是候选 token 不在，而是树结构把它们接错了。

SABA（State-Aware Budget Allocation）解决固定预算失配。作者把每轮 throughput 看成 accepted gain 与 verification overhead 的函数，学习一个按当前 decoding state 选择 tree budget 的策略。直觉上，easy round 本来 acceptance 就长，再加节点收益很快饱和；hard round 适度扩树更值钱，但也不是越大越好。SABA 用当前轮的状态特征预测不同 budget 下的收益，选 throughput 最优预算。这里的关键不是复杂控制器，而是把“树大小”从静态超参变成在线决策变量。

整体上，GRAFT 没有改 DLM 本身，也没有改 target verification 机制，而是专门优化 DLM-based tree assembly 这一层。这种切分是合理的，因为瓶颈正出在 one-pass future distributions 到 executable tree 的映射。e:T781,GRAFT has two components: TDES and SABA.

TDES, or Target-Distilled Edge Scoring, addresses parent-child mismatch. DDTree is effectively parent-agnostic: a child token contributes the same score regardless of which parent path it extends, because scoring is driven by per-position token probabilities. GRAFT instead learns an explicit edge scorer distilled from target-model traces. The scorer takes candidate parent-child pairs from adjacent positions, together with contextual features, and predicts how compatible that transition is with the target model. Tree assembly then uses these edge scores, rather than only the product of marginal token probabilities, to select target-compatible paths under a node budget.

Mechanistically, this is a lightweight structural predictor that compresses local target preferences into a cheap model. It does not rerun the target and does not force the DLM drafter into autoregressive expansion. It patches the missing conditional dependency at the tree-assembly stage. That is the right intervention point, because the failure mode is not merely missing candidate tokens; it is incorrect attachment among already available candidates.

SABA, or State-Aware Budget Allocation, addresses budget misallocation. The paper models per-round throughput as a trade-off between draft gain and verification cost, then chooses the budget conditioned on the current decoding state. Easy rounds already have long accepted prefixes, so larger trees mostly add cost. Hard rounds benefit more from extra nodes, but only up to a saturation point. SABA predicts the utility of different budgets from state features and selects the throughput-optimal one online.

Overall, GRAFT leaves the DLM and the target verification mechanism unchanged. It focuses specifically on the mapping from one-pass future distributions to an executable draft tree. That decomposition is sensible and technically clean.f:T4a2,The main empirical claim is consistent end-to-end throughput improvement with very small overhead. The paper reports 2.13x to 6.36x throughput gains over baselines across models and datasets, while adding less than 0.5 ms overhead per decoding round. That matters because it suggests the method is not trading away the cost structure of speculative decoding.

The ablations are more informative than the headline numbers. TDES improves MAT, indicating that parent-child compatibility is a real bottleneck for DLM-based tree construction. The paper shows both examples and aggregate evidence that target-compatible tokens are often already present among candidates, but become unusable because they are connected to the wrong parent. SABA shows that a fixed budget is not robust: the throughput-optimal tree size varies substantially across rounds. Dynamic allocation preserves most of the acceptance gain while avoiding unnecessary verification cost.

From a systems perspective, the strongest result is not a single benchmark number but the combination of stable gains across tasks and low added latency. That makes the method more credible as a practical layer on top of DLM drafters.10:T455,This is a skim paper, but the introduction, method section, and ablations are worth reading carefully.

Focus on three things. First, the two failure modes defined in the introduction: parent-child mismatch and budget misallocation. If those are convincing, the rest of the paper follows naturally. Second, inspect how TDES gets supervision from target traces and what features the edge scorer actually uses. The important question is what information is added beyond marginal token probabilities. Third, check whether SABA is optimizing throughput directly or only a proxy such as acceptance length.

For figures and tables, prioritize the mismatch examples, the MAT/throughput comparisons, and the curves showing diminishing returns with larger budgets. Expected reading time is about 25 to 35 minutes. If reading selectively, the problem setup, the two modules, and the ablations are enough. If reading more deeply, two follow-up questions are worth keeping in mind: how target-specific the distilled edge scorer is, and whether the budget policy can generalize to broader adaptive verification strategies.11:T4ff,The method, DPQ (Doubt-Preserving Quantization), is a family of pre-quantization calibration-data recipes rather than a new quantizer. The pipeline is lightweight. First, run the full-precision model on candidate examples. Then select target-aware 'fragile' examples using FP uncertainty signals. Two signal families matter most: high-doubt examples, such as high entropy, low confidence, or small top-2 margin; and answerability-boundary examples, especially for answer/no-answer settings like SQuAD2. These are mixed with generic anchor examples and then passed to an unchanged quantizer such as GPTQ or AWQ. The theoretical argument is modest but useful: if the deployment target puts more mass near decision boundaries, while the calibration mixture is dominated by generic anchors, the optimal calibration set will differ from the one preferred by broad MCQA-style targets. The paper calls this mixture mismatch. The framing is built around two preservation risks: a distributional risk that measures deviation between quantized and FP predictive distributions, and a boundary risk that measures decision flips in low-margin regions, optionally reweighted toward answerability or abstention-heavy inputs. This is a better fit for real systems than accuracy-only calibration.12:T539,The evaluation spans 8 language models, 9 NLP benchmarks, and 22 comparison methods or configurations. The metric suite is broader than standard quantization papers: besides ECE, adaptive ECE, NLL, and Brier score, the authors report FP-behavior metrics such as FP agreement, JSD between quantized and FP predictive distributions, confidence shift, top-two margin drift, and margin correlation. On SQuAD2 they also evaluate answerability-boundary behavior directly. Three findings stand out. First, the best calibration recipe depends strongly on the target. For boundary-sensitive answerability preservation, a high-boundary mixture such as DPQ-s128-r75 works best. For broad MCQA, milder recipes such as DPQ-r50 or single-signal confidence-only / entropy-only selection are often better. Second, accuracy can hide substantial uncertainty drift: some settings preserve top-1 behavior while materially changing confidence and margin structure. Third, preservation signals transfer only partially across quantizers. The post-hoc calibration and AWQ analyses suggest that some useful signals are not tied to one quantizer, but the best score-preservation recipe still remains quantizer-specific. The paper is less about one headline average gain and more about establishing the robustness of this target dependence across models and tasks.13:T758,系统结果是全文最硬的部分。作者在 NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell GPU 上报告，优化后的 BF16 实现从 4K 上下文开始超过 dense attention；在 32K 时，单层 attention 达到 10.91× speedup。更重要的是，真实的八层 retrofit 在 warm 32K whole-model TTFT 上带来 15.3% 的下降。这个数字没有单层 10× 那么夸张，但更可信，因为它把 attention 之外的层、调度、kernel launch、planning 等开销都算进去了。

训练结果方面，论文做了三种 matched Stage A 对照：Dense continued training、equal-pair-count local sliding、equal-pair-count static-random nonlocal graph。BF1 在三次训练 seed 下都排第一。这里最关键的不是“第一”这个标签，而是控制做得比较细：local sliding 和 random graph 都匹配了 pair budget，因此 BF1 的优势更可能来自拓扑结构，而不是单纯看得更多。换句话说，作者试图回答的是“在相同稀疏预算下，dyadic + first-block 这种图是否比局部或随机图更适合 next-token LM”。

论文还单独讨论了 selected-page decode semantics，并指出在低 batch 场景下，主瓶颈不是 selected-page attention arithmetic，而是 planning lifecycle。这个观察很重要，因为它解释了为什么很多稀疏 decode 方案在 paper benchmark 上很快，落到真实 serving 却收益有限：算子省下来的时间被 page selection、mask planning、runtime orchestration 吃掉了。

从当前给出的正文片段看，底座是 Qwen3-0.6B 几何配置。若全文有 perplexity 或 downstream LM 指标，阅读时要重点看 BF1 retrofit 后相对 dense-continued-training 的差距是否稳定很小，以及这种差距是否随上下文长度扩大。如果长上下文收益建立在明显牺牲短上下文 LM 质量之上，那方法的适用面会窄很多。14:T723,BF1 is a deterministic, block-aligned causal sparse-attention pattern. The sequence is partitioned into blocks of width b, with the reported public configuration using b = 64. For a query block j, the route includes four components: the causal self block, two exact local predecessor blocks, the first global block, and historical predecessor blocks at dyadic distances, i.e. j - 2^r starting from an offset of four blocks. In short, it is local + global-first + dyadic history.

The paper is explicit that this route is related to earlier log-sparse and dilated designs such as LogSparse Transformer and LongNet. The claimed novelty lies elsewhere: pretrained-model retrofit, matched topology-control experiments, and correctness-gated Blackwell measurements. The correctness-gated part is important. The authors do not only benchmark a fast path; they verify partial blocks, backward gradients, dense-path equivalence, future-token isolation, and opposite-order benchmarks up to 32K, which makes the systems claims more credible.

With fixed block width, each converted layer uses O(n log n) selected token interactions and has O(log n) graph communication depth. The communication-depth argument is the structural reason BF1 should beat pure local sliding windows: long-range information can propagate in logarithmically many sparse hops rather than linearly many local layers. Compared with static random nonlocal graphs, BF1 is deterministic and easier to optimize at the kernel level.

Another sensible design choice is that the paper does not replace all attention layers in the model and call it done. It reports an actual eight-layer retrofit separately from an all-layer timing-only ceiling. That makes the results more realistic for pretrained models, where replacing every layer with a sparse operator can be risky.15:T74e,The strongest evidence is on the systems side. On an NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell GPU, the optimized BF16 implementation reportedly crosses dense attention around 4K context length. At 32K, the per-layer attention operator reaches 10.91× speedup. More importantly, the actual eight-layer retrofit reduces warm whole-model TTFT by 15.3% at 32K. That number is much smaller than the per-layer speedup, but it is also much more informative because it includes the rest of the model and runtime overheads.

For language modeling, the paper runs three matched Stage A training comparisons: dense continued training, equal-pair-count local sliding, and equal-pair-count static-random nonlocal graphs. BF1 ranks first in all three matched runs. The key point is not the ranking alone. The controls are designed to keep the sparse budget comparable, so the advantage is more plausibly due to topology rather than simply attending to more pairs. In other words, the paper tries to answer whether a dyadic-plus-first-block graph is a better sparse inductive bias for next-token LM than local-only or random sparse graphs under similar budget.

The decode analysis is also useful. The paper reports selected-page decode semantics and argues that, at low batch, the dominant unresolved cost is planning lifecycle rather than selected-page attention arithmetic. That observation explains why sparse decode methods often look strong in operator benchmarks but deliver limited gains in real serving stacks.

From the provided text, the tested geometry is based on Qwen3-0.6B. When reading the full paper, the most important thing to check is how much LM quality is lost relative to dense continued training, and whether that gap stays small across context lengths. If long-context efficiency comes with a noticeable short-context LM penalty, the deployment envelope becomes narrower.16:T43b,A good skim order is: operator definition, systems measurements, then matched training controls. Start with the route definition and complexity proposition to understand exactly which blocks are connected and why the selected interactions are O(n log n) with O(log n) communication depth. Then jump to the systems section and focus on the dense crossover point, the 32K per-layer speedup, and the whole-model TTFT for the eight-layer retrofit. Finally, read the matched Stage A training results to see whether topology, sparse budget, and training adaptation are properly disentangled.

If you only have about 25 minutes, prioritize Figure 1, the complexity statement, the correctness-gated Blackwell benchmark tables, and the matched-control training results. Three details are worth deeper inspection: why the first block is used as the global anchor instead of learned global tokens, which eight layers were retrofitted and why, and how the planning-lifecycle overhead is broken down during decode. That last point may be more operationally important than the sparse route itself.17:T885,方法核心是 trace → execution graph → workload synthesis → deterministic replay → profiling 这一整条链路。

1. 通用 tracing。XPerf 支持对 LangChain、LangGraph 等常见 agent 框架做无侵入或低侵入埋点；对自建 agent 只需很少注释。它记录的不只是 LLM 请求本身，还包括工具调用及其时间信息。

2. 执行图重建。论文用基于 time-span tree 的分析方法，从 traces 中恢复 LLM call、tool call 及其依赖关系，得到 execution graph。这个设计比简单按时间顺序回放更合理，因为 agent 负载里存在并发、等待工具返回、条件分支等结构，只有显式依赖图才能保留真实调度约束。

3. 工作负载合成。作者没有把 benchmark 限定为“原样回放历史 trace”，而是允许在 execution graph 之上改变请求到达模式，构造 online serving、offline batch inference、fixed-concurrency 等不同场景。这一点很关键，因为系统研究需要控制 arrival process，才能比较调度器、batching、cache 策略在不同负载区间的行为。

4. 可复现回放。为保证 replay fidelity，XPerf 对每个 LLM call 提交记录下来的精确 input token IDs，而不是重新 tokenize 文本；这样可以保留 prefix cache 命中行为。必要时还可以强制 serving engine 解码记录过的 output tokens，避免因为模型输出偏移而改变后续控制流。回放时还遵守 execution graph 的依赖顺序，并把工具调用时长纳入时间轴，因此即便被测 serving engine 与 tracing 时不同，负载形态仍尽量贴近原始应用。

5. 细粒度 profiling。系统层面报告 KV cache usage、prefix cache hit rate、token throughput 等；硬件层面报告 GPU tensor core 利用率、memory bandwidth 利用率等。作者强调 profiler 开销很低，避免 benchmark 本身扭曲结果。

和已有工作相比，区别主要在两点：一是它不是只测独立请求，也不是只做 agent 任务完成率；二是它公开、可扩展，并且把“非确定性 agent 行为”通过 trace replay 固定下来，而不是接受 run-to-run variance 作为噪声。18:T896,The method is a full pipeline: trace collection, execution-graph reconstruction, workload synthesis, deterministic replay, and low-overhead profiling.

1. Application-agnostic tracing. XPerf instruments popular agent frameworks such as LangChain and LangGraph with minimal friction, and supports lightweight annotations for custom agents. It records both LLM calls and tool calls, including timing information.

2. Execution-graph construction. Rather than replaying requests as a flat sequence, XPerf reconstructs dependencies among LLM calls and tool invocations using a time-span-tree-based analysis. This matters because agent workloads contain waiting, branching, and sometimes parallel structure. A dependency graph preserves the scheduling constraints that actually shape serving behavior.

3. Workload synthesis. XPerf does not stop at replaying historical traces. It can alter request arrival patterns over the reconstructed execution graphs to emulate online serving, offline batch inference, and fixed-concurrency settings. This makes the framework useful for controlled systems studies, where arrival processes must be varied independently of application logic.

4. Reproducible replay. For each LLM call, XPerf submits the exact recorded input token IDs, not just the raw text, so prefix-cache behavior is preserved. It can also force the serving engine to decode the recorded output tokens, preventing output drift from changing downstream control flow. Replay respects dependency order in the execution graph and accounts for tool-call durations, so the workload remains realistic even when the target serving engine differs from the one used during trace collection.

5. Fine-grained profiling. XPerf reports serving metrics such as KV-cache usage, prefix-cache hit rate, and token throughput, and hardware metrics such as tensor-core utilization and memory-bandwidth utilization. The profiler is designed to add negligible overhead.

Relative to prior work, the distinction is clear: it is neither an agent-quality benchmark nor a standard serving microbenchmark. Its central idea is to freeze the stochastic control flow of agent applications into a reproducible systems workload.19:T5aa,The evaluation uses eight agentic applications spanning deep research, coding, Q&A, and general-purpose assistance. The most important results fall into three buckets.

First, replay fidelity. XPerf achieves below 3% mean absolute percentage error in end-to-end request latency for both isolated and concurrent requests. That is the key validation result: if replay were not faithful, the rest of the benchmark would be hard to trust.

Second, workload variance in native agent runs. The paper quantifies how unstable agent execution can be without replay. The headline example is the roughly 70% average variation in the number of LLM calls for the same request under mini-SWE-agent with gpt-oss-120b. This result justifies the entire benchmark design.

Third, bottleneck diagnosis. With replay fixed, XPerf can decompose end-to-end latency and relate it to prefix-cache hits, KV-cache occupancy, token throughput, and GPU compute or bandwidth utilization. The paper’s emphasis is not on claiming one serving engine wins universally, but on showing that the framework can localize where performance is lost under agent-style traffic.

For a foundation-model audience, the useful takeaway is that agent workloads materially change prefix reuse, request-length distributions, and concurrency structure. Optimizations that look effective on standard chat-style benchmarks may behave differently once execution graphs become deeper and more irregular.1a:T46f,The conceptual move is to extend JND from human perception to machine receivers. Instead of asking whether a compressed signal is visually indistinguishable, the paper asks whether the downstream policy response remains effectively unchanged. In a VLA setting, the receiver is a language-conditioned robotic policy and the response is a continuous action vector, including translation, rotation, and gripper control. Compression decisions are therefore guided by action sensitivity rather than by visual similarity or attention mass. Based on the introduction, the method likely defines an action-conditioned noticeability metric for visual tokens or intermediate features, estimates a tolerance boundary or compression budget per token, and uses that budget for pruning, reuse, or feature compression. The main contribution appears more conceptual than architectural: safe compression should be defined by receiver behavior. The exact formulation, how action deviation thresholds are estimated, whether an auxiliary calibration model is used, and where compression is applied in the stack would need the full method section to verify.1b:T42f,The evaluator state is decomposed into three typed sources: grounds for evidence, norms for decision rules, and authority for whether a rule applies to the case. For an old and revised evaluator state, each source can be taken from either version, producing a 2^3 intervention table called the judgment cube. Each hybrid state is executed through a trusted adjudicator. A source replacement set is a reason when it is sufficient to reproduce the revised verdict from the old state, and it is kept only if it is inclusion-minimal. The complete set of such minimal sufficient replacements is the judgment receipt. A key design choice is the separation between prediction and certification: a model can emit a receipt, but only execution of the counterfactual hybrids certifies it. The work also introduces paired consistency as an evaluation primitive. Control transformations carry known metamorphic relations; when a pair violates the relation, at least one prediction must be wrong. This gives an error lower bound without needing gold labels for every transformed item.1c:T818,MGAL is built from long-form reports in the UN Digital Library, using position-aligned multilingual documents so that cross-lingual comparisons are not merely task-level but also semantically and structurally aligned. It covers Arabic, Chinese, English, French, Russian, and Spanish, with context lengths from 8K to 128K tokens. The benchmark spans four linguistic granularities—word, sentence, paragraph, and document—and includes both closed-ended and open-ended tasks. The paper mentions seven tasks in total, including word-level QA, sentence-level cloze, paragraph filling, document summarization, and translation, with 420 query-response pairs per task per language.

The key design choice is controlled stratification by evidence position. Examples are labeled by whether the relevant evidence appears at the beginning, middle, or end, indexed at both document and paragraph levels. This turns the benchmark into a diagnostic instrument rather than a generic task suite. The choice of UN reports is practical: they provide multilingual parallelism, topic consistency, and relatively regular discourse structure, which makes coherent unit definitions more reliable.

Quality control is manual and strict. The authors state that every query-response pair was audited and flawed samples were removed. This is a smaller-but-cleaner benchmark philosophy, which is appropriate for diagnostic evaluation.

Scoring is hybrid. Closed-ended tasks use precision-oriented reference metrics such as Accuracy, while open-ended tasks use ROUGE-L and, for tasks like paragraph filling where lexical overlap is insufficient, an LLM-as-a-judge setup. That trade-off is sensible: pure reference metrics under-credit semantically correct paraphrases, while pure judge-based evaluation reduces reproducibility. MGAL uses both.

Relative to LongBench, LV-Eval, M4LE, and ONERULER, the novelty is not simply broader coverage. The main difference is tighter control over variables that matter for error attribution: multilingual alignment, discourse granularity, and evidence position.1d:T8dd,The experimental section is useful mainly for the error patterns it exposes. The benchmark is evaluated on 12 long-context LLMs. The main findings reported in the paper fall into four groups.

First, performance degrades as granularity becomes coarser. Models do well on word-level tasks, but performance drops at sentence, paragraph, and document levels. This suggests that many current long-context models are stronger at local matching or retrieval than at stable discourse-level integration.

Second, multilingual gaps widen in lower-resource languages. Large open-source and closed-source models are relatively comparable on higher-resource languages, but closed-source systems maintain a clearer lead on lower-resource ones, and smaller open models degrade more sharply. For pretraining, this points to data coverage and multilingual representation quality as major bottlenecks, not just architecture.

Third, position sensitivity remains strong. In sentence cloze, blanks appearing earlier in the text produce the highest error rates, with performance improving toward later positions, consistent with recency-biased attention. The authors also observe an early-option bias: models disproportionately choose earlier answer options such as A or B, indicating reliance on answer-order heuristics rather than evidence verification.

Fourth, the paper identifies two more specific failure modes. One is local semantic crowding: when neighboring sentences share entities, years, or discourse markers, models over-rely on surface overlap and pick the wrong sentence, failing to distinguish functional roles such as background, explanation, or outcome. The other is a fluency-consistency gap: generated outputs are often fluent and stylistically plausible but weakly anchored to the source, leading to factual drift and unsupported claims.

In terms of benchmark scale, MGAL covers 6 languages, 7 tasks, 4 granularities, and contexts from 8K to 128K, with 420 examples per task per language. Even without chasing a single aggregate score, this is enough to support meaningful slicing by granularity and position. The most informative numbers are likely the relative slopes across word→sentence→paragraph→document and begin→middle→end, not the overall ranking.1e:T936,UpgradeBench 的设计核心是 longitudinal，而不是静态 pairwise transfer。作者沿着四代 Qwen release 加上一条 continuation release，构造了真实的升级路径；同时补充 OLMo checkpoints，因为 OLMo 的训练关系是公开且可验证的，可以把“是否来自同一训练轨迹”当成受控变量，而不是靠 release note 猜。

它把升级决策拆成四种策略：freeze、port、refresh、retrain。freeze 是继续部署旧 specialist；port 是把旧 adapter 或 task vector 直接迁到新 base；refresh 是用旧 specialist 作为 teacher，对新 base 做便宜的再专门化；retrain 则是从头按固定 recipe 重训 specialist。围绕这四种策略，作者定义并测量了几个关键量：retention、recovery cost、specialization half-life，以及 upgrade tax。这里最重要的是 retention：新策略相对“该 hop 上可达到的最佳 specialist”保留了多少收益。这样定义后，不同 hop、不同任务、不同模型规模可以放到同一坐标系里比较。

任务设置覆盖 6 个应用任务、3 类任务、2 个模型尺度，并把训练侧成本纳入 benchmark 输出，包括 GPU-minutes 和 energy。这个点做得比较扎实，因为很多迁移论文只报最终精度，不报恢复到同等质量需要多少代价，而实际部署决策恰恰取决于这个成本。

方法上它没有提出新的迁移算法，贡献在于实验设计和变量控制。尤其是对 port 的分析很关键：作者直接检验一个业界常见假设——只要新旧模型架构兼容，adapter 原样拷贝就是强基线。实验结论是否定的。决定能否直接拷贝的不是 shape compatibility，而是训练连续性，也就是两个 checkpoint 是否位于同一权重演化轨迹上，以及相距多远。

OLMo 部分进一步把这个结论做成了更接近机制层面的测量。沿同一 documented trajectory 往后走，adapter retention 会随着 continuation token 数增加而下降；文中给出的边界是，从 46B-token continuation 时仍有 0.88–0.99 retention，走到 2.9T-token continuation 时则掉到接近 no-adapter floor。后续 anneal 和 model-soup merge 并没有带来额外主要损伤。这个结果很有信息量，因为它把“能不能 port”从模糊经验变成了 distance budget 的问题。1f:T8e6,实验规模不算大，但设计很完整：总共 98 次训练、193 个 evaluation cells，全部在一张 RTX 4090 上完成，并释放了 per-query prediction、bootstrap CI 和 energy log。这个设置说明作者更关心可复现的决策测量，而不是堆大算力。

几个关键结果值得记住。第一，specialist 不一定会很快过时。对于 intent classification，freeze 在 9 个 upgrade hops 上能保留 99%–101% 的 attainable gain；跨四代模型，retrained specialist 的参考分数只移动了 0.2 分，而 zero-shot 却提升了 21 分。这说明某些任务的专门化收益主要来自标签体系、输出格式或任务数据本身，base 进步并不会立刻吞掉这些收益。

第二，另一些任务则明显更依赖 base 能力。text-to-SQL 上，冻结旧 specialist 在单个 hop 上最多会损失 59% 的 attainable gain，而且它相对新 base 的优势会在三代内衰减到零。作者据此提出 data-bound 与 base-bound 的区分：前者 specialist moat 更稳，后者随着 base reasoning 能力增强会更快被追平。

第三，naive porting 可能非常危险。文中给了一个很强的反例：在两个架构完全相同但来自独立预训练运行的 checkpoint 之间，直接贴 adapter 会把 intent classification 从 92.8% 打到 42.9%，甚至低于不用 adapter 的 60.7%。但如果目标模型是同一权重继续预训练得到的 descendant，同样的操作可以保持接近 reference-level accuracy。这一组结果基本否定了“同家族同结构即可直接迁移”的经验法则。

第四，OLMo 验证复现了同样规律，并进一步定位了边界：同一训练轨迹上，随着 continuation 距离增大，port retention 从 0.88–0.99 逐步跌到 no-adapter floor；而 anneal、model soup merge 不是主要破坏源。这个结论对 checkpoint 发布和 continual pretraining 很有现实意义。

文中还给出了一个 half-life 估计：在 7–8B 规模，specialization half-life 大约落在 2–3 个 generation；在 1.5–1.8B 规模，至少观察到一次 crossing，即新 base 的便宜采用策略真正超过旧 specialist。这个量化虽然依赖任务集，但比过去泛泛讨论“要不要升级”要扎实得多。20:T955,The main design choice is longitudinal evaluation rather than static pairwise transfer. The benchmark follows four consecutive Qwen releases plus one continuation release, creating a realistic upgrade path. It also includes OLMo checkpoints, where training relationships are documented rather than inferred, allowing the authors to treat training continuity as a controlled variable.

The benchmark evaluates four upgrade strategies: freeze, port, refresh, and retrain. Freeze keeps serving the old specialist. Port copies the old adapter or task vector onto the new base. Refresh uses the old specialist as a teacher to cheaply re-specialize the new base. Retrain rebuilds the specialist from scratch under a fixed recipe. Around these strategies, the paper defines and measures retention, recovery cost, specialization half-life, and upgrade tax. Retention is especially important: it measures how much of the attainable gain on the target hop is preserved by a cheaper strategy relative to the best specialist achievable there. This makes comparisons across hops, tasks, and model scales much more meaningful.

The benchmark covers six application tasks from three classes, at two model scales, and treats training-side cost as a first-class output, including GPU-minutes and energy. That is a strong design choice because deployment decisions depend on cost-to-recover, not just final accuracy.

The paper does not introduce a new transfer algorithm. Its contribution is experimental structure and variable control. The most important analysis concerns porting. A common practitioner assumption is that if two checkpoints are shape-compatible, direct adapter copying is a strong baseline. The paper shows that this assumption is false. What licenses copying is not architectural shape but training continuity: whether the target checkpoint lies on the same weight trajectory, and how far away it is.

The OLMo experiments sharpen this into something close to a mechanistic statement. Along the same documented trajectory, adapter retention decays as continuation distance grows. The paper reports retention of 0.88–0.99 after a 46B-token continuation, but near no-adapter-floor performance after a 2.9T-token continuation. Subsequent annealing and model-soup merging add little extra damage. This turns portability from a vague family-level intuition into a distance-budget question.21:T9cb,The experimental scale is modest but well structured: 98 trainings and 193 evaluation cells, all run on a single RTX 4090, with per-query predictions, bootstrap confidence intervals, and energy logs released. The emphasis is clearly on reproducible decision measurement rather than brute-force benchmarking.

Several results stand out. First, specialists do not always become obsolete quickly. On intent classification, freezing retains 99%–101% of the attainable gain across nine upgrade hops. Over four generations, the retrained-specialist reference score moves by only 0.2 points, while zero-shot performance rises by 21 points. This suggests that some specialist value is tied mainly to task data, label taxonomies, or output conventions rather than to frontier base capability.

Second, other tasks are much more base-dependent. On text-to-SQL, the frozen specialist can forfeit up to 59% of attainable gain on a single hop, and its advantage over a newer base decays to zero within three generations. This motivates the paper’s data-bound vs base-bound distinction: some specialist moats are stable, others are rapidly eroded by improvements in the base model’s reasoning ability.

Third, naive porting can fail catastrophically. The paper gives a strong counterexample: between two architecturally identical checkpoints from independent pretraining runs, directly pasting the adapter drops intent classification from 92.8% to 42.9%, which is even worse than the 60.7% obtainable with no adapter at all. But when the target is a continued-pretraining descendant of the same weights, the same operation preserves near-reference accuracy. This directly challenges the common rule of thumb that same-family, same-shape transfer is safe.

Fourth, the OLMo validation reproduces the same pattern and localizes the boundary more precisely. Along the same training trajectory, port retention falls from 0.88–0.99 to the no-adapter floor as continuation distance increases; annealing and model-soup merge are not the main sources of damage. This is highly relevant for continual pretraining and checkpoint release strategy.

The paper also estimates a specialization half-life of roughly two to three generations at the 7–8B scale, and observes at least one actual crossing at 1.5–1.8B where cheap adoption of the new base overtakes the old specialist. The exact values are task-dependent, but this is much more concrete than the usual qualitative discussion around whether upgrades are worth it.22:T49b,Read this one with a concrete deployment or training question in mind. Start with the introduction and Figure 1, where the paper lays out the three intuitions it challenges: specialists do not always age quickly, porting is not always safe, and task differences are not well captured by a generative/discriminative split. Then read the benchmark setup and metric definitions carefully, especially retention, recovery cost, and specialization half-life. Without those definitions, the later conclusions can look like anecdotal observations.

In the experiments, prioritize the strategy comparisons across tasks and the evidence from both Qwen and OLMo supporting the claim that continuity, not architectural shape, governs portability. If you work on continual pretraining, checkpoint selection, adapter ecosystems, or practical model deployment, this is worth a full read. If you only need the main conclusions, 40–60 minutes on the main paper should be enough. The section around the independent-pretraining-run vs continued-pretraining-descendant comparison is the most important one to study closely, because it directly probes the limits of parameter-space transferability.23:T5a3,The method is an agentic LLM-as-judge setup. The data consists of pairs of revisions for the same declaration from Mathlib PRs: an earlier version that was sent back for revision, and the later version that was accepted. Importantly, each version is scored independently. The judge does not know the score of the other revision and is not explicitly told it is doing pairwise comparison, which reduces comparison leakage. The rubric decomposes proof quality into five dimensions: library leverage, automation fit, structural clarity, statement quality, and Mathlib conventions. Each dimension is scored from 1 to 10, then combined by a harness into a final score. The key design choice is tool grounding. The judge can query the exact Mathlib state at the relevant commit through bash, search for existing lemmas, inspect signatures, and check available APIs. That lets it reason like a human reviewer: is this proof reinventing something already in the library, should the statement be more general, is there a cleaner one-line invocation already available. This is materially different from a text-only judge because proof quality often depends on library context, not just the local proof text. For dataset construction, the authors used Claude Sonnet 5 to inspect historical PRs and identify cases with substantial revision differences that were not just linting or newly added declarations, then split them into development and test sets.24:T5dc,The benchmark contains 218 declaration pairs in the test split and 123 pairs in the dev split used for rubric tuning. Six judges were evaluated, including three open-weight and three closed models, with three replicates per judge. Each evaluation allows up to 20 tool calls per sample to cap inference cost. The main metric is preference alignment: whether the judge assigns a higher score to the revision that Mathlib ultimately accepted. All models perform well above the 50% random baseline. Claude Sonnet 5 is best at 80.8%, while even DeepSeek-V4-Flash reaches 63.5%, with all sign tests reported at p < 1e-5. Among open models, muse-glimmer-30b gets 70.2% and inkling-small 69.3%, showing that the task is not exclusive to frontier closed models. The most useful experimental finding is the variance analysis. Re-running the same judge on the same declarations flips roughly one-fifth to nearly half of the verdicts. Some ablations look non-significant in one replicate, p = 0.34, and significant in another, p = 0.006. That means single-run judge benchmarks can easily overstate differences. The PR 11640 case study is also informative: the initial proof reconstructed a result already present in Mathlib, while the merged revision replaced it with a one-line call to H.restrictPreimage s. The judge found the existing declaration via tool use and consistently penalized the initial version on library leverage. This kind of semantic review cannot be replicated by linting or regex heuristics.25:T9bd,Libra 的核心设计是 decoupled vision-language system：一套 vision system，一套 language system，中间用 cross-modal bridges 连接。关键不只是双塔，而是在每层内部把 self-modal modeling 和 cross-modal interaction 分成两种计算模式。

第一是 switch attention。作者区分两种场景：模态内部建模时，用标准 attention；跨模态交互时，不直接让一种模态去对齐另一种模态的原始特征空间，而是先通过 cross-modal bridge 把 Q/K/V 映射到新的交互空间，再做注意力。这么做的动机很明确：如果直接共享同一注意力空间，视觉和语言会互相拉扯，尤其在数据不平衡时更容易让弱势模态被强势模态同化。bridge 相当于给跨模态交互加了一个受控接口，保留各自表示的自治性。

第二是 switch FFN。它对应不同任务场景下的后续变换分流，用来进一步区分理解与生成、以及自模态与跨模态信息融合后的处理方式。虽然从摘要和引言看，具体实现细节需要结合方法章节确认，但设计意图很清楚：attention 负责信息路由，FFN 负责场景化变换，二者共同完成“共享框架下的计算解耦”。

在实例化上，作者给了两个版本。Libra-1 面向 understanding-only image-to-text。这里有两个值得注意的点：一是 supervision 采用 unified discrete autoregression，把视觉和语言都放进离散 AR 框架里，优先换取训练稳定性；二是 vision input 采用 hybrid tokenization，把连续视觉信号和离散 ID 结合起来，试图兼顾细粒度感知与离散序列训练的稳定性。这个选择说明作者并不完全相信纯离散视觉 token 足够表达感知细节。

Libra-2 扩展到 unified understanding + generation。按引言描述，它在 tokenization、representation、supervision 上又加了额外策略，目标是同时支持 image-to-text 和 text-to-image。这里最关键的不是“能不能统一”，而是统一后是否还能维持两边能力不互相伤害。Libra 的回答是通过结构分流降低干扰，而不是完全依赖数据配比去硬平衡。

如果和典型 unified MLLM 比，Libra 的区别在于它不把跨模态交互视为默认状态，而把它视为一种需要显式开关和专门映射空间的计算模式；如果和 cascade 比，它又没有把视觉生成完全外包给独立大后端，而是保留了端到端联合学习的可能。26:Ta8b,Libra is a decoupled vision-language system with one vision branch and one language branch connected by cross-modal bridges. The key idea is not merely having two towers, but introducing two computation modes inside the model: self-modal modeling and cross-modal interaction.

The first component is switch attention. For self-modal modeling, Libra uses standard attention. For cross-modal interaction, it does not directly align one modality into the raw feature space of the other. Instead, queries, keys, and values are mapped through a cross-modal bridge into a dedicated interaction space before attention is computed. The motivation is clear: direct sharing of the same attention space can cause one modality, usually language, to dominate the other under imbalanced data. The bridge acts as a controlled interface that preserves modality-specific representations while still allowing information exchange.

The second component is switch FFN. It further separates computation after attention depending on the scenario, helping distinguish self-modal processing from cross-modal fusion, and likely also understanding from generation. The exact implementation details need the method section, but the design intent is straightforward: attention handles routing, FFN handles scenario-specific transformation, and together they realize decoupled computation within a unified framework.

The paper instantiates the framework in two versions. Libra-1 targets understanding-only image-to-text modeling. Two details matter here. First, supervision uses unified discrete autoregression over both vision and language, prioritizing training stability. Second, vision inputs use hybrid tokenization, combining continuous visual signals with discrete IDs to balance fine-grained perception and stable sequence modeling. This suggests the authors do not fully trust purely discrete visual tokens to carry enough perceptual detail.

Libra-2 extends the framework to joint multimodal understanding and generation. According to the introduction, it adds further strategies in tokenization, representation, and supervision to support both image-to-text and text-to-image. The central claim is not simply that these tasks can be unified, but that they can be unified without one side damaging the other, thanks to structural decoupling rather than only careful data balancing.

Compared with typical unified MLLMs, Libra treats cross-modal interaction as an explicit computation mode with dedicated mappings, not as the default state of every layer. Compared with cascade systems, it does not outsource visual generation to a separate large backend, so it preserves the possibility of end-to-end joint learning.27:T597,From the available full-text fragment, the experiments are organized in two settings: Libra-1 for multimodal understanding and Libra-2 for joint understanding and generation. The paper claims that the architecture is effective in both settings and that the evaluation also covers tokenization, representation, and supervision choices.

However, the provided content does not include the later experimental tables, so it is not possible to responsibly report exact model sizes, training tokens, dataset scales, or precise gains over baselines. What can be stated with confidence is that the paper evaluates not only understanding-only settings but also unified generation settings, which is more complete than many works that stop at captioning or VQA. The separate Libra-1 and Libra-2 instantiations also indicate an important methodological point: a unified architecture does not imply a single training recipe; understanding-only and understanding-plus-generation require different supervision and tokenization choices.

If the full experiments include ablations, the most important ones to inspect are: removing switch attention or switch FFN, comparing hybrid tokenization against purely discrete or purely continuous visual inputs, and measuring whether unified training introduces a clear trade-off between understanding and generation quality. Without those ablations, the architectural claim would be much harder to assess.28:T619,This is a skim-to-careful-read paper if you care about native multimodal pretraining rather than downstream VQA scores alone.

Start with the introduction and the architecture overview. The main point is not just building an MLLM that can generate images, but addressing training bias under modality imbalance in unified systems. Then read the switch attention and switch FFN sections carefully. In particular, check what the cross-modal bridge actually is: an independent parameter space, a low-rank mapping, or a gated projection. That determines whether the gain comes from inductive bias or simply from added capacity.

Next, compare Libra-1 and Libra-2. The important part is not the naming but how supervision, tokenization, and losses differ between understanding-only and joint understanding-generation settings. If reading selectively, prioritize the architecture figure, the tokenization design, the main result tables, and any ablations directly tied to modality imbalance. Expected reading time is about 35 to 50 minutes.

Three deeper questions are worth keeping in mind. First, whether hybrid tokenization is merely an engineering patch or evidence that current discrete visual tokenizers are still insufficient for unified AR modeling. Second, whether the switch modules help because they impose a better inductive bias or because they isolate parameters and reduce negative transfer. Third, whether this decoupled design still holds at much larger data scale, or mainly serves as a practical compromise under current multimodal data limitations.29:T476,Re$^3$Cap treats multimodal retrieval as a semantic constraint mechanism.
1) Image-to-dataset retrieval: given an image v, retrieve Top-K similar image-text pairs R_v from a paired corpus D using an image retrieval model.
2) CRS (Caption Refinement Suggester): extract semantic elements that consistently appear across the retrieved captions; these act as “must-preserve / should-include” anchors.
3) CQA (Caption Quality Assessor): use the generated caption as a query to retrieve another set R_c; discrepancies between R_v and R_c are interpreted as hallucinations (caption introduces content that shifts retrieval away from the image-based neighborhood) or omissions (missing details that prevent alignment).
4) RL training uses CRS+CQA outputs to guide caption refinement, encouraging generation of new candidates rather than merely scoring existing ones.
Key differences vs common RL captioning: it avoids relying on a single VLM/CLIP scalar judge, does not require extra keyword annotations, and aims to reduce reward hacking by grounding feedback in retrieval-set consistency—assuming the retrieval model and corpus are reliable.2a:T485,The framework, Multi-Turn Certified Robustness (MTCR), models conversation as a State-Adversarial MDP. Dialogue history is mapped to a continuous state; at each turn an adversary chooses a user message from an epsilon-bounded perturbation set; the LLM responds according to its policy; and the state is updated. Safety is defined by a predicate over the state space. The central quantity is k-turn certified robustness: the worst-case probability of staying safe for k turns under any adaptive adversary. A naive certificate gives a lower bound of p^k, where p is the worst-case single-turn certified safety probability. MTCR tightens this through embedding-space mode decomposition, separately certifying intra-mode safety and inter-mode transitions. It also introduces (alpha, beta)-safety persistence, a structural condition under which the degradation can improve from p^k to beta^k when beta is larger than the worst-case single-turn bound, with a linear characterization 1 - k(1 - beta) for interpretable horizon estimates. The paper also discusses tightness for non-overlapping decompositions and impossibility results without structural assumptions.2b:T49c,The dataset contains 40 Korean conference sessions, 19 medical and 21 finance, with multilingual segments preserved when present. Each condition produces 2,598 to 2,765 QA items, a roughly 6% range. The main grid covers 9 models from five families, EXAONE, Gemma, Llama, Phi, and Qwen, ranging from 2.4B to 70B parameters. The main experiment has 72 LoRA runs, plus 12 full fine-tuning sanity runs and an 8-cell Whisper-medium STT-swap grid. The LoRA recipe uses rank 16, alpha 32, dropout 0.05, 4-bit QLoRA, learning rate 2e-4, 3% warmup, 3 epochs, and effective batch size 16. Evaluation includes KMMLU, KMMLU-Pro, MMLU, four LLM judges, six blind human experts, STT substitution, and an LLM difficulty audit. QA quality improves by +0.18 according to LLM judges and +0.22 according to humans on a 1–5 scale, but the cross-model mean MCQA gain is not significant. Gains concentrate in model-family and domain-aligned settings. Replacing the internal STT system with Whisper-medium changes aggregate KMMLU by at most 1.32 percentage points on average. The difficulty audit finds that two strong LLM judges still cannot answer 7.8% of the public-sample QA items with confidence.2c:T7ca,The setup is built around Qwen3-8B as a hybrid model. The same base model can be decoded in think mode, where it generates reasoning before deciding, or in no-think mode, where it emits a direct action token. This allows a relatively controlled comparison between a reasoning policy and a direct classifier without changing the model family. The task is binary intervention timing: given a prefix of a multi-party conversation, decide whether an assistant should speak now or remain silent. The dataset contains about 16,000 synthetic conversations expanded into roughly 173,000 token-level decision points, with only about 13% positive intervention labels, so the class imbalance is severe. The paper compares several training routes. One is direct decision-token SFT, which tends to produce a stronger classifier but suppresses reasoning. Another is a masked objective that preserves the model’s native reasoning, but only on cases the model already solves correctly; this means it receives little useful signal on failure cases and therefore struggles to improve beyond the base reasoning policy. A third route is think-mode RL, optimizing either accuracy or a behavioral faithfulness reward. The authors point out a key limitation of group-relative RL objectives: when all sampled rollouts agree, there is no learning signal, even if they all agree on the wrong answer. That leaves many confidently wrong prompts untouched. For auditing, the paper combines activation probes with behavioral interventions. Probes test when the decision becomes decodable in the hidden states, while interventions remove or replace parts of the generated reasoning to see whether the final action changes, which is a more causal test of whether reasoning matters. The paper also argues that several common metrics are misleading in this setting, including probability-based contribution scores, probe accuracy that ignores class imbalance, probing only at the end of reasoning, and uncontrolled flip tests.2d:T613,The main empirical result is that the strongest action policy is the direct classifier that does not explicitly generate reasoning, while the reasoning policy is more auditable but less accurate, with the largest cost coming from missed interventions. That is the central capability–auditability tradeoff. In training experiments, decision-token SFT further strengthens the direct policy but pushes reasoning toward irrelevance. The masked objective cannot substantially beat the base reasoning policy because it only preserves reasoning on examples the model already gets right. Think-mode RL also fails to clearly surpass the base reasoning policy, not because RL is useless in general, but because the group-relative objective gives no gradient on prompts where all sampled rollouts agree, including confidently wrong ones. The audit results are also informative. For the reasoning policy, the eventual decision becomes decodable during the chain of thought, whereas for the direct classifier the decision is already decodable at the prompt stage. That supports the interpretation that the direct policy commits early, while the reasoning policy at least partially forms the decision during reasoning. The authors also stress that single-run comparisons can confuse seed variance with reward-objective effects, so multiple seeds or replicates are necessary when comparing RL variants. The excerpt provided here does not include the full numeric tables, but the paper is clearly organized around mechanism evidence rather than a single headline score.2e:T588,The architecture has three parts: a frozen backbone LLM, a safety-specific LoRA adapter, and a learned gate that outputs a continuous routing score g in [0,1] from hidden-state representations. The adapter is not simply turned on or off. Its strength is scaled continuously inside the model, with the effective transformation written as h' = (W + gΔW)h. This makes the method closer to interpolation between the base model and a safety-adapted model, rather than hard switching or post-hoc filtering.

A key design choice is that the gate is trained jointly with the safety adapter. It is not an external moderation classifier. The gate directly modulates internal computation during generation. The paper adds two training ingredients to make routing discriminative. First, subtype-aware gate weighting gives different emphasis to different harmful and benign prompt categories, aiming to reduce false positives on safety-adjacent benign inputs. Second, a hard pairwise margin objective explicitly separates harmful and benign prompts in gating space. This is important because plain generation loss may not force a clean routing boundary. Compared with standard safety LoRA, the novelty is not the adapter itself but the input-conditioned continuous activation. Compared with guard-model pipelines, the novelty is that routing happens inside the model’s latent computation rather than as an external pre-filter.2f:T755,The evaluation covers safety with HarmBench and XSTest, and utility with GSM8K, MMLU, and TruthfulQA, using Llama-3-8B-Instruct and Gemma-2-2B-it as the main backbones. The paper’s case is not built on one isolated metric. It is built on a better trade-off point.

On Llama-3-8B-Instruct, CLEAR reportedly drives HarmBench attack success rate close to zero. Against existing aligned variants in the Llama-3 and Alpaca families, CLEAR reaches 0.50% ASR, while the compared aligned variants remain around 15.50% to 24.00%. At the same time, it retains 73.46% on GSM8K, about 7 points higher than globally applied SFT and standard LoRA. That is the most important result in the paper because it suggests the utility gain is not coming from weaker safety, but from selective intervention.

On Gemma-2-2B-it, the same pattern appears and is arguably more informative for smaller models: GSM8K improves from roughly 38% under SFT/LoRA to 42%, and TruthfulQA MC2 rises from about 47% to 53%. This supports the idea that smaller models suffer more from global safety tuning and therefore benefit more from conditional routing.

XSTest is used to probe routing quality rather than end-task generation alone. The hidden-state gate, with fewer than 1M parameters, is claimed to achieve a favorable harmful-versus-benign routing trade-off. That matters because it distinguishes CLEAR from simply adding another classifier in front of the model.

The most important ablations to inspect are the comparisons against always-on LoRA, removal of the pairwise margin objective, and removal of subtype-aware weighting. The central question is whether utility preservation truly comes from conditional routing rather than from a weaker safety adapter. If the paper shows CLEAR maintaining similar or better ASR while preserving noticeably higher GSM8K or TruthfulQA, then the argument is strong.30:T412,Read it as a routing paper, not as another safety fine-tuning paper. Start with the method figure and the equation for gated adapter injection to verify that the gate modulates internal LoRA strength rather than acting as an output filter. Then read the training objective carefully, especially the subtype-aware weighting and pairwise margin terms, because those are the pieces that make the gate more than a soft classifier. Finally, inspect the main tables where HarmBench is shown alongside GSM8K and TruthfulQA. The key question is whether CLEAR preserves utility at comparable safety, not whether it simply relaxes refusal behavior.

If time is limited, 20 to 30 minutes is enough for a solid skim: Introduction for the alignment-tax framing, method section for the gate and losses, and the main result tables plus XSTest analysis. Two details are worth deeper inspection if available in the full text: which hidden states feed the gate, and whether the routing score is prompt-level, layer-specific, or token-dynamic during generation.31:T55b,The method is a deterministic short-window probe. Starting from the last two tokens, the model predicts the next token by argmax; the window is rolled forward and the process is iterated, producing a discrete dynamical system. From 96 random two-token starts, the authors measure the fixed-point fraction φ, the proportion of trajectories that terminate in a fixed point. They also classify trajectory geometry into funnel, none, fragmented, and borderline, which gives a coarse structural summary of the attractor landscape. Prefixes are added as wrappers to every forward pass without changing the estimator, so the observed effect is attributed to the conditional distribution rather than to the readout definition. The paper compares raw, BOS, and ordinary text prefixes across six models and sweeps window size. The interesting part is that the paper is organized around failed factorizations. Prefix length is not a monotone driver. Two proposed content-side factors fail after widening the sample. Model-side hypotheses such as a universal direction, bidirectionality as a model property, and instruct-resistance also fail. Even the nearest mechanistic account, attention-sink strength, matches the sign of the fixed-point shift in only 2 of 5 models. What remains is pairwise and model-conditioned: the same prefix can push four models toward 0 and two toward 1.32:T61a,Training is modeled as a deterministic discrete-time dynamical system over a complete state S_t=(w_t,m_t,v_t,\tau_t), where w are parameters, m the first moment (momentum/Adam first-moment buffer), v the second moment, and \tau auxiliary deterministic state (clock/schedule).
- Forward sensitivity (transport): define q_t=\partial S_t/\partial \alpha for a source-strength parameter \alpha. The recurrence includes (a) injected source perturbations d_t (teacher-induced gradient changes) and (b) feedback through \partial G/\partial S because past perturbations alter w_t and therefore future gradients.
- Backward sensitivity (valuation): define a terminal observer O (in LLMs, a target-minus-reference candidate-string log-likelihood on a frozen prompt bank). Compute the costate p_T=(\partial O/\partial S_T)^\top and propagate it backward to obtain a per-step “future value” \lambda_t capturing how valuable a state perturbation at time t is for the final behavior.
- Exact transport–valuation identity: total behavioral change is expressed as a path integral pairing forward-propagated perturbations with backward-propagated values, making route dependence explicit.
- State surgery for causal identification: by transplanting components of the trainer state at a cut, they show the first moment m is the main carrier that continues to write source ancestry into weights after source removal. Transplanting m alone can change future trajectories while keeping current parameters/outputs unchanged; recovering terminal behavior requires (w,m) together.33:T492,CARD has three steps. First, for each axis such as belief, intent, or knowledge, the authors train L2-regularized logistic probes on per-layer, per-head activations at the final input token. Each example contains a matched statement and its negation, and the learned unit vector points from the negated statement toward the matched one. Second, they select the top K=56 layer-head pairs by held-out probe accuracy, roughly 7% of all heads, and use forward hooks to add a scaled version of the probe direction only at the final-token position and only in those head slices. The steering scale is alpha=+10 or alpha=-10 in units of the activation standard deviation along the probe direction. Third, they measure a different prediction axis after steering, for example steering belief while evaluating action. The main statistics are the true-belief versus false-belief accuracy gap shift and a mean-accuracy shift. The accompanying Relay Chain benchmark is a cooperative grid-world setup with belief, knowledge, intent, and action questions, plus success and counter partitions that separate constant-prior behavior, world-state tracking, and belief-conditional behavior.34:T584,The work has two parts. First, a diagnostic analysis. On four ordinal benchmarks and four MLLM backbones, the authors probe frozen hidden states across layers and prompt protocols to test whether ordered labels are linearly recoverable. They then go beyond probe scores and analyze how the unembedding matrix filters the ordinal direction, and how much of that direction is retained by the digit-token row subspace W_S. This turns the gap between probe-readability and generation into a concrete output-subspace issue. Second, they propose Ordinal Lens Alignment (OLA), a frozen-backbone inference-time correction method. OLA does not modify the vision encoder, LM backbone, or original unembedding. It trains lightweight lenses on K mid-to-deep decoder layers, with the lenses anchored to the digit-token row space, produces layer-wise ordinal distributions, fuses them with learned softmax weights into an instance-specific P_OLA, and then applies a discrepancy-driven correction to logits only at the answer position and only over the target digit-token set. The design choices are deliberate: multi-layer lenses because ordinal structure emerges across depth and varies by instance; W_S anchoring because the goal is to reconnect latent evidence to the native output interface rather than to maximize free probe accuracy; and target-restricted correction to avoid disturbing the full vocabulary distribution.35:T491,The strongest evidence is in the diagnostic section. Under the most informative setup, task-designed prompt plus last-token hidden state, all backbone-dataset combinations achieve probe SRCC of at least 0.756, with peaks including 0.938 on Adience with Qwen3-VL, 0.919 on Aesthetic with Gemma-4, and 0.843 on HCI with Qwen3-VL. In contrast, the digit-token row space retains less than 1.15% of the ordinal-direction energy across all 16 model-dataset pairs, which strongly supports the output-bottleneck claim. At the behavioral level, there is a 16 to 77 absolute-point accuracy gap between hidden-state linear probes and native digit-token outputs. That is large enough to argue that the main issue is not the absence of latent ordinal evidence. In method comparisons, OLA outperforms prompt-only, hidden steering, capacity-matched baselines, and offline-lens variants, and beats the LoRA-tuned OrderChain baseline in most settings while keeping the backbone frozen. For a closer read, the most useful checks are whether ACC, MAE, and SRCC improve consistently, and how much each component matters: number of layers K, W_S anchoring, and target-restricted correction.36:T4ec,The paper proposes and documents the Salt method as a disciplined workflow rather than a new learning algorithm.
- Fixed deliverable bundle per iteration: (i) implementation, (ii) formal specification, (iii) kernel-checked proof that the implementation meets the spec, (iv) tests including adversarial controls to ensure the proof has “bite,” and (v) certificates—simplified restatements of the spec designed for human comprehension.
- The key contract is implication: the kernel proves the formal statement entails the certificate statement, so the certificate can only be weaker than what was proved. This makes statement-level human review tractable without trusting the full formal development.
- Emphasis on specification risk: correctness is meaningless if the spec is wrong; therefore the workflow centers on active interrogation, where the human queries the system and receives kernel-checked evidence (unfolding binders, justifying hypotheses, producing variants under changed assumptions).
- End-to-end verified stack culminating in a RISC-V tapeout, with strict provenance (commit-verified theorem trails) and append-only error logs.
The design choice is to optimize for controllable autonomy at AI speed, not for minimal formalization effort.3:["$","main",null,{"className":"flex-1 Among many variants, score-entropy discrete diffusion (SEDD) has achieved particularly strong empirical results. In SEDD, new samples are generated by iteratively evaluating a sequence of concrete score functions, which are learned by minimizing a score-entropy loss.   While much of the prior theoretical literature on discrete diffusion has focused","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"must_read","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-08-21T00:16:00Z","category_id":"architecture","cover_url":"/covers/2608_20635.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["stat.ML","cs.LG"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这项工作研究 SEDD 中 concrete score 估计的统计极限：给定有限训练样本，学习离散扩散反向过程所需的密度比 p_t(y)/p_t(x) 到底需要多少样本，score-entropy loss 下最好的可能误差是多少。它把问题从“采样器在 score 足够准时是否收敛”推进到“score 本身在有限样本下能否被可靠估计”。","importance":"这是离散 diffusion LM 理论中比较核心的一块。SEDD、masked diffusion LM、LLaDA 一类非自回归语言建模方法，最终都要面对离散状态上的 denoising / score estimation 问题。已有理论更多分析初始化误差和离散化误差，通常把 score estimation error 当作给定条件。这里直接给出 minimax 下界，并构造接近该下界的估计器，说明 score-entropy 目标的统计代价来自哪里。对预训练研究的启发是：离散扩散不只受采样步数限制，也受 token 分布稀疏性、transition graph、时间噪声分布和密度比估计难度限制。语言数据上的长尾 token 和稀有上下文会使这个问题更尖锐。","technical_approach":"论文以 CTMC 离散扩散为框架。前向过程由 transition rate matrix 定义，在每个时间 t 诱导分布 p_t。反向过程需要 concrete score，也就是所有状态对上的密度比 p_t(y)/p_t(x)。SEDD 用 score-entropy loss 学这个比值：对样本 x 和候选跳转 y，用由 transition rate 决定的权重 w_yx 加权 Bregman divergence D(a,b)=a/b-1-log(a/b)，其中 b 是真实密度比，a 是模型估计。\n\n核心技术贡献是把 concrete score estimation 作为一个统计估计问题处理。先分析单个分布 p 下的 score 函数估计，给出 minimax lower bound；再给出一个简单估计器，通常可理解为基于经验频率的 plug-in 估计，并配合平滑或截断来避免低概率状态上的比值爆炸。上界与下界在主要项上匹配，可能只差常数或对数因子。随后把单时刻结果接到 SEDD 的时间依赖情形，用加权积分形式控制 E_SE，也就是总 KL 误差分解中的 score estimation 项。这个路线和研究 τ-leaping、uniformization、Gillespie、Euler 等采样器误差的工作互补：那些工作问“已知 score 后怎么采样更准”，这里问“有限样本能把 score 学到多准”。","experiments":"这是理论论文，没有常规 LLM 训练实验，也没有模型参数量、语料规模或 benchmark 性能数字。关键结果是定理而不是实验曲线。全文围绕三类误差展开：初始化误差 E_init 通常随时间 horizon T 指数衰减；离散化误差 E_dis 由采样算法和步长决定；score estimation error E_SE 由有限样本学习 concrete score 带来。论文的主要结果是给出 E_SE 相关的 minimax 下界，并证明一个简单估计器可近似达到该界。消融意义上的洞察是：当 p_t(x) 很小或状态空间/transition neighborhood 变大时，密度比估计会变难；这说明离散 diffusion LM 的统计瓶颈可能集中在低概率 ratio 的定义。核心应放在 minimax lower bound 的定理、对应 estimator 的 upper bound，以及从单时刻 score estimation 推到时间积分 E_SE 的部分。证明细节可以选择性读，但要关注下界构造使用了哪些困难分布，以及上界如何处理小概率状态；这些点最容易转化为对 tokenizer、噪声日程、transition matrix Instead of assuming that the learned score is accurate, it asks how well the density ratios p_t(y)/p_t(x) can be estimated from data under the score-entropy loss.","importance":"This is directly relevant to non-autoregressive language modeling based on discrete diffusion. Prior theory for SEDD often decomposes the sampling error into initialization, discretization, and score-estimation terms, then focuses mainly on the first two. This work isolates the third term and characterizes its minimax difficulty. For language modeling, the message is clear: discrete diffusion is constrained not only by sampler design, but also by the statistical hardness of estimating ratios over sparse, long-tailed token distributions.","technical_approach":"$a","experiments":"There are no standard empirical LLM experiments in the provided content. The paper also frames these results through the usual SEDD error decomposition: initialization error decays with the diffusion horizon, discretization error depends on the sampler, and the score-estimation error is the finite-sample statistical term. The practical implication is that rare states and large transition neighborhoods make ratio estimation harder, which is especially relevant for text where token distributions are highly imbalanced.","reading_guide":"Read closely in about 1.5–2 hours. Existing sampling-based watermarking methods typically inject position-wise i.i.d. perturbations, which can be poorly aligned with DLM decoding dynamics and degrade generation quality. Gumbel 扰动，而是构造“平滑、局部自相关”的 Gumbel 扰动场（SAC: Smooth Auto-Correlated）。具体做法是用 Gaussian copula 生成具有指定局部相关结构的联合分布，同时在边缘上保持 Gumbel marginals（在文中假设下）。直觉是：DLM 每步会并行更新多个位置，邻近位置的预测需要在多步中逐渐一致；i.i.d. 的高频扰动会让相邻位置在迭代中互相“打架”，导致振荡或局部不连贯。\n- 机制分析：用“latent perturbation roughness / local-difference energy”刻画扰动场的高频成分，给出从 i.i.d. 到相关场后粗糙度下降的解析表达（文中写到 D_iid=2，D_SAC=2(1-ρ^2 r1) 这类形式），用来支撑“更匹配迭代精炼”的假设。\n- 检测端：提出 SAC-aware detector。核心是协方差感知的滤波（covariance-aware filtering）去读取由相关扰动诱导的证据几何；并用 native-sample calibration 做校准以控制误报。另提供 global-offset scan，用于在 token–signal 轻微错位时做粗同步恢复（更像诊断/稳健性补丁）。\n整体上，它不是改 DLM 训练，而是改 DLM 解码时的 keyed randomness 注入方式，并把检测器做成“匹配滤波器”。","experiments":"实验平台是 LLaDA。作者声称相对“matched 的 i.i.d. 2","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-08-21T09:49:09Z","category_id":"architecture","cover_url":"/covers/2608_20927.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CL","cs.AI"],"summary":{"core_problem":"论文处理的是跨模型 的基线。","importance":"这篇工作的启发点在于，它把 latent guidance 的失败原因从“student 能力不足”里拆出来，单独识别为“guidance freshness”问题。对预训练和模型压缩研究有两个具体价值：第一，说明大模型到小模型的 latent transfer 不是一次性 prefill 就能覆盖长程生成，接口需要随生成状态更新；第二，它把 prefill-decode 分离扩展到跨模型服务场景，大模型承担低频更新，小模型承担高频解码，这对长文本低成本生成有实际参考意义。贡献更偏系统化方法和诊断，不是新的语言建模目标。","technical_approach":"方法分两步。先用训练无关的 OracleSwap 诊断 staleness：每隔固定 token 数，让 mentor 重新 prefill 输入加已生成前缀，重建并替换 memory；student 权重、已生成文本和 KV cache 都不变。这样能只改变 guidance 内容的新鲜度，避免把收益误归因到额外训练或解码重启。正式方法 MentorPulse 使用一个有容量上限、带位置信息的 slot memory 压缩 mentor states，student 通过 gated cross-attention 读取。refresh 时复用 mentor KV cache，只处理新增 token，然后替换 memory，不使 student 的历史 KV 失效。训练上引入 Windowed Refresh Training：对采样到的 memory 版本，只优化对应输出窗口，让 bridge 学会在不同 freshness 状态下读取信息。refresh interval R 控制质量和成本，R 趋于无穷时退化为 one-shot guidance。","experiments":"诊断实验使用 Qwen3.5-27B mentor 和 Qwen3.5-4B student。Multi-IF 上，直接读 mentor states 的 probe 显示 staleness 很明显：A_stale 从 t=0 的 0.79 降到 t=512 的 0.47，而 A_fresh 维持在 0.76 到 0.80。行为层面，静态 guidance 在多轮指令跟随中使 4B student 的约束满足率比无 guidance 基线低 2.5 分；每 16 token 刷新一次 memory 后，变成比无 guidance 基线高 2.0 分。fresh 但不匹配的 memory 不带来收益，支持问题来自内容是否匹配当前前缀。正式评测覆盖 13 个数据集，主模型对上 MentorPulse 恢复了 mentor-student gap 的 52.2%，约为最强 prior alternative 的两倍。优势主要出现在长输出任务；短输出往往还没到第一次 refresh，收益有限。能力差距过大的 mentor-student pair 上收益会收窄，论文用 64-step read-distribution variance V64 作为部署前筛选信号。吞吐测量中，默认 R=16 在 R=8、16、32 里处于较好的质量-成本折中，并在长输出任务上相对文本 guidance 更有成本优势。","reading_guide":"泛读建议 25 到 35 分钟。优先看第 3 节的 staleness 诊断和 Figure 1、Figure 2，那里是论文最有信息量的部分：它证明固定 latent memory 在长生成中会从帮助变成干扰。随后看方法部分关于 versioned slot memory、incremental mentor prefill、Windowed Refresh Training 的设计，重点理解为什么 refresh memory 不需要重置 student KV cache。实验部分重点看长输出任务、不同 refresh interval 的质量-成本曲线，以及 V64 如何预测 pair 是否适合部署。附录里 probe 设置和 byte-level refresh accounting 值得快速检查，它们决定诊断是否站得住。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper studies a failure mode of cross-model latent guidance for long-form generation. Prior methods encode the prompt once with a large frozen mentor and let a smaller frozen student read the resulting latent signal throughout decoding. That static signal can become stale as the generated prefix grows. In long outputs, the student’s next decision depends on the prompt plus its own generated history, while the mentor memory still describes only the initial prompt.","importance":"The main value is the clean separation between capability limits and guidance freshness. The work shows that latent transfer from a large model to a small model is not simply a one-shot prefill problem when generation is long. The interface must track the evolving prefix. This matters for model compression and serving: a large model can provide low-frequency latent updates, while a small model performs most decoding steps. The contribution is mainly a diagnostic and serving-oriented method rather than a new pretraining objective.","technical_approach":"The paper first uses a training-free intervention called OracleSwap. Every fixed number of generated tokens, the mentor re-prefills the prompt plus current generated prefix, rebuilds the memory, and swaps it in. The student weights, generated text, and student KV cache are left unchanged, so the intervention isolates the effect of memory freshness. MentorPulse then makes this practical. Mentor states are compressed into a capped, position-aware slot memory. The student reads this memory through a gated cross-attention bridge. Refreshing is versioned and incremental: the mentor reuses its KV cache and processes only newly generated tokens, then replaces the memory without invalidating the student’s decoding state. Windowed Refresh Training trains the bridge on output windows associated with sampled memory versions. The refresh interval R sets the quality-cost trade-off; one-shot guidance is recovered as R goes to infinity.","experiments":"$c","reading_guide":"Skim time: about 25–35 minutes. Start with the staleness diagnosis in Section 3 and the first two figures; that is where the paper makes its strongest empirical point. Then read the method pieces on versioned slot memory, incremental mentor prefill, and Windowed Refresh Training, paying attention to why the student KV cache is not reset. In the experiments, focus on long-output tasks, refresh-interval trade-offs, and the V64 predictor. The appendices on probe construction and byte-level refresh accounting are worth checking if the deployment claim matters.","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"这类 frozen mentor 引导常被默认可一次编码到底，这篇把“指导信号会过期”说清了；先看刷新频率消融和额外 mentor compute 账，若收益不只是算力换分，就值得在长输出蒸馏里复现。","en":"People often assume frozen mentor guidance can be encoded once and reused forever; this paper makes the staleness failure mode explicit.
